[user] Memorize and track the chain(s) of variable assignment hidden in the following text.

[The relevant text is in a separate document accessible via the read_chunk tool — see the system prompt for usage.] (Document length: 3997 tokens.)
Question: Find all variables that are assigned the value 98715 in the text above.

Reply with the answer inside \boxed{}.
[assistant] This document is 3997 tokens — too long to read in one context. This task is order-dependent: a variable is only ever as correct as the chain that assigns it, so the document has to be processed sequentially, in document order, not in parallel. I therefore process the document from the start with a running accumulator: read the first slice and update the accumulator by tracking every variable assigned in the range and noting the latest assignment to each variable (overwriting the previous one), then hand the rest of the document plus the accumulator to a subagent to continue the same way. Once the final slice is folded in, the accumulator holds the whole-document result and I turn it into the final answer.

[assistant] This document is 2296 tokens — too long to read in one context. The result over a range does not depend on the order of the examples, so disjoint ranges can be computed independently and merged. The question asks about labels within user ID groups, so the state is a per-(user, label) tally, not a per-label one. I therefore split the range in half recursively, having a subagent compute the per-(user, label) tally (how many examples have each user ID AND label combination) over each half and combining the two partials. Once I have it for the whole document, I turn the combined result into the final answer.
